Mathlib.Geometry.Convex.Cone.Basic

ConvexConestructureMathlib.Geometry.Convex.Cone.Basic, type
Wording
convex cone of R and M (from the name)
English
Let $R$ and $M$ be sets. Then the convex cones of $R$ and $M$ are a basic type.
Formula
$R , M \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \mathrm{ConvexCone} (R , M)\ \mathsf{set}$
French
Soient $R$ et $M$ des ensembles. Alors les cônes convexes de $R$ et $M$ sont un type de base.
ConvexCone.BluntdefMathlib.Geometry.Convex.Cone.Basic, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $C$ be a convex cone of $R$ and $M$. Then $\operatorname{Blunt}$ holds for $C$, if $0$ is not an element of $C$.
Formula
$C \in \mathrm{ConvexCone} (R , M)\vdash \operatorname{Blunt} (C):\Leftrightarrow \neg (\MembershipmemMACRoAA {C}{0})$
French
Soit $C$ un cône convexe de $R$ et $M$. Alors $\operatorname{Blunt}$ tient pour $C$ si $0$ n'est pas un élément de $C$.
ConvexCone.FlatdefMathlib.Geometry.Convex.Cone.Basic, predicate
Wording
C is flat (from the docstring)
English
Let $C$ be a convex cone of $R$ and $G$. Then $C$ is flat, if there exists an element $x_{4}$ of $G$, such that $x_{4}$ is an element of $C$, $x_{4} \neq 0$ and $\MembershipmemMACRoT {C}{\operatorname{neg} (x_{4})}$.
Formula
$C \in \mathrm{ConvexCone} (R , G)\vdash \operatorname{Flat} (C):\Leftrightarrow \exists x_{4} \in G . \MembershipmemMACRoAA {C}{x_{4}}\wedge (x_{4} \neq 0 \wedge (\MembershipmemMACRoAA {C}{\operatorname{neg} (x_{4})}))$
French
Soit $C$ un cône convexe de $R$ et $G$. Alors $C$ est plat s'il existe un élément $x_{4}$ de $G$ tel que $x_{4}$ soit un élément de $C$, $x_{4} \neq 0$ et $\MembershipmemMACRoT {C}{\operatorname{neg} (x_{4})}$.
ConvexCone.IsReproducingdefMathlib.Geometry.Convex.Cone.Basic, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $C$ be a convex cone of $R$ and $M$. Then $\operatorname{IsReproducing}$ holds for $C$, if $C - C$ is equal to the universal set.
Formula
$C \in \mathrm{ConvexCone} (R , M)\vdash \operatorname{IsReproducing} (C):\Leftrightarrow \EqtMACRoJ {C - C}{\operatorname{univ}}$
French
Soit $C$ un cône convexe de $R$ et $M$. Alors $\operatorname{IsReproducing}$ tient pour $C$ si $C - C$ est égal à l'ensemble universel.

ConvexCone.SalientdefMathlib.Geometry.Convex.Cone.Basic, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $C$ be a convex cone of $R$ and $G$. Then $\operatorname{Salient}$ holds for $C$, if for all elements $x_{4}$ of $G$, if $x_{4}$ is an element of $C$, then if $x_{4} \neq 0$, then $\operatorname{neg}$ applied to $x_{4}$ is not an element of $C$.
Formula
$C \in \mathrm{ConvexCone} (R , G)\vdash \operatorname{Salient} (C):\Leftrightarrow \forall x_{4} \in G . \MembershipmemMACRoAA {C}{x_{4}}\rightarrow x_{4} \neq 0 \rightarrow \neg (\MembershipmemMACRoAA {C}{\operatorname{neg} (x_{4})})$
French
Soit $C$ un cône convexe de $R$ et $G$. Alors $\operatorname{Salient}$ tient pour $C$ si pour tous les éléments $x_{4}$ de $G$, si $x_{4}$ est un élément de $C$, alors si $x_{4} \neq 0$, alors $\operatorname{neg}$ appliqué à $x_{4}$ n'est pas un élément de $C$.
ConvexCone.carrierdefMathlib.Geometry.Convex.Cone.Basic, function or constant
Wording
the carrier of self (from the name)
English
Let $\mathrm{self}$ be a convex cone of $R$ and $M$. Then the carrier of $\mathrm{self}$ is an element of $\wp M$ defined as $\operatorname{proj0}$ applied to $\mathrm{self}$.
Formula
$\mathrm{self} \in \mathrm{ConvexCone} (R , M)\vdash \operatorname{carrier} (\mathrm{self}):= \operatorname{proj0} (\mathrm{self})\in \wp M$
French
Soit $\mathrm{self}$ un cône convexe de $R$ et $M$. Alors le carrier de $\mathrm{self}$ est un élément de $\wp M$ défini comme $\operatorname{proj0}$ appliqué à $\mathrm{self}$.

Wording
the copy of C and s (from the name)
English
Let $C$ be a convex cone of $R$ and $M$. Let $s$ be an element of $\wp M$ such that $\EqtMACRoH {s}{C}$. Then the copy of $C$ and $s$ is a convex cone of $R$ and $M$.
Formula
$C \in \mathrm{ConvexCone} (R , M), s \in \wp M , \EqtMACRoJ {s}{C}\vdash \operatorname{copy} (C , s , \mathrm{hs})\in \mathrm{ConvexCone} (R , M)$

ConvexCone.hulldefMathlib.Geometry.Convex.Cone.Basic, function or constant
Wording
the cone hull of R and s (from the docstring)
English
Let $R$ be a set. Let $s$ be an element of $\wp M$. Then the cone hull of $R$ and $s$ is a convex cone of $R$ and $M$ defined as the infimum of $\operatorname{ofPred}$ applied to the function that maps $C$ to $\mathrm{Leq} (s , C)$.
Formula
$R \in \mathrm{Set} , s \in \wp M \vdash \operatorname{hull} (R , s):= \operatorname{sInf} (\operatorname{ofPred} (\lambda C . \mathrm{Leq} (s , C)))\in \mathrm{ConvexCone} (R , M)$
French
Soit $R$ un ensemble. Soit $s$ un élément de $\wp M$. Alors l'enveloppe de cônes de $R$ et $s$ est un cône convexe de $R$ et $M$ défini comme l'infimum de $\operatorname{ofPred}$ appliqué à la fonction qui envoie $C$ à $\mathrm{Leq} (s , C)$.

Submodule.toConvexConedefMathlib.Geometry.Convex.Cone.Basic, function or constant
Wording
the convex cone of C (from the name)
English
Let $C$ be a submodule of $R$ and $M$. Then the convex cone of $C$ is a convex cone of $R$ and $M$ defined as $\operatorname{mk}$ applied to $C$, the function that maps $c$, $h$, $x_{4}$ and $\mathrm{hx}$ to $\mathrm{Submodule\_\_smul\_mem}$ applied to $C$, $c$ and $\mathrm{hx}$ and the function that maps $x_{5}$, $\mathrm{hx1}$, $x_{6}$ and $\mathrm{hy}$ to $\mathrm{Submodule\_\_add\_mem}$ applied to $C$, $\mathrm{hx1}$ and $\mathrm{hy}$.
Formula
$C \in \mathrm{Submodule} (R , M)\vdash \operatorname{toConvexCone} (C):= \operatorname{mk} (C , \lambda c , h , x_{4} , \mathrm{hx} . \mathrm{Submodule\_\_smul\_mem} (C , c , \mathrm{hx}), \lambda x_{5} , \mathrm{hx1} , x_{6} , \mathrm{hy} . \mathrm{Submodule\_\_add\_mem} (C , \mathrm{hx1} , \mathrm{hy}))\in \mathrm{ConvexCone} (R , M)$
French
Soit $C$ un sous-module de $R$ et $M$. Alors le cône convexe de $C$ est un cône convexe de $R$ et $M$ défini comme $\operatorname{mk}$ appliqué à $C$, la fonction qui envoie $c$, $h$, $x_{4}$ et $\mathrm{hx}$ à $\mathrm{Submodule\_\_smul\_mem}$ appliqué à $C$, $c$ et $\mathrm{hx}$ et la fonction qui envoie $x_{5}$, $\mathrm{hx1}$, $x_{6}$ et $\mathrm{hy}$ à $\mathrm{Submodule\_\_add\_mem}$ appliqué à $C$, $\mathrm{hx1}$ et $\mathrm{hy}$.

Mathlib.Geometry.Convex.Cone.Dual

PointedCone.dualdefMathlib.Geometry.Convex.Cone.Dual, function or constant
Wording
the dual cone of p and s (from the docstring)
English
Let $p$ be a linear map of $M$ and the set of linear maps of $N$ and $R$. Let $s$ be an element of $\wp M$. Then the dual cone of $p$ and $s$ is a pointed cone of $R$ and $N$.
Formula
$p \in \mathrm{LinearMap} (\operatorname{id} (R), M , \mathrm{LinearMap} (\operatorname{id} (R), N , R)), s \in \wp M \vdash \operatorname{dual} (p , s)\in \mathrm{PointedCone} (R , N)$
French
Soit $p$ une application linéaire de $M$ et l'ensemble d'applications linéaires de $N$ et $R$. Soit $s$ un élément de $\wp M$. Alors le cône dual de $p$ et $s$ est un cône pointé de $R$ et $N$.

Mathlib.Geometry.Convex.Cone.DualFinite

PointedCone.DualFGdefMathlib.Geometry.Convex.Cone.DualFinite, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $p$ be a linear map of $M$ and the set of linear maps of $N$ and $R$. Let $C$ be a pointed cone of $R$ and $N$. Then $\operatorname{DualFG}$ holds for $p$ and $C$, if there exists a finite set $s$ of $M$, such that the dual cone of $p$ and $s$ is equal to $C$.
Formula
$p \in \mathrm{LinearMap} (\operatorname{id} (R), M , \mathrm{LinearMap} (\operatorname{id} (R), N , R)), C \in \mathrm{PointedCone} (R , N)\vdash \operatorname{DualFG} (p , C):\Leftrightarrow \exists s \in \mathrm{Finset} (M). \EqtMACRoJ {\operatorname{dual} (p , s)}{C}$
French
Soit $p$ une application linéaire de $M$ et l'ensemble d'applications linéaires de $N$ et $R$. Soit $C$ un cône pointé de $R$ et $N$. Alors $\operatorname{DualFG}$ tient pour $p$ et $C$ s'il existe un ensemble fini $s$ de $M$ tel que le cône dual de $p$ et $s$ soit égal à $C$.

English

Formula

PointedCone.Face.prodOrderIsodefMathlib.Geometry.Convex.Cone.Face.Lattice, function or constant
Wording
the face lattice of the product of two cones of C and D (from the docstring)
English
Let $C$ be a pointed cone of $R$ and $M$. Let $D$ be a pointed cone of $R$ and $N$. Then the face lattice of the product of two cones of $C$ and $D$ is an order isomorphism of the set of faces of the product of $C$ and $D$ and $\mathrm{Prod}$ applied to the set of faces of $C$ and the set of faces of $D$.
Formula
$C \in \mathrm{PointedCone} (R , M), D \in \mathrm{PointedCone} (R , N)\vdash \operatorname{prodOrderIso} (C , D)\in \mathrm{OrderIso} (\operatorname{Face} (\operatorname{prod} (C , D)), \mathrm{Prod} (\operatorname{Face} (C), \operatorname{Face} (D)))$
French
Soit $C$ un cône pointé de $R$ et $M$. Soit $D$ un cône pointé de $R$ et $N$. Alors le [DepNoun] de $C$ et $D$ est un isomorphisme d'ordres de l'ensemble de faces du produit de $C$ et $D$ et $\mathrm{Prod}$ appliqué à l'ensemble de faces de $C$ et l'ensemble de faces de $D$.

PointedConedefMathlib.Geometry.Convex.Cone.Pointed, type
Wording
pointed cone of R and E (from the name)
English
Let $R$ and $E$ be sets. Then a pointed cone of $R$ and $E$ is an element of the function that maps $R$ and $E$ to the set of submodules of $\mathrm{Nonneg}$ applied to $R$ and $E$.
Formula
$R , E \in \mathrm{Set} \vdash \mathrm{PointedCone} (R , E):= \lambda R , E . \mathrm{Submodule} (\mathrm{Nonneg} (R), E)$
French
Soient $R$ et $E$ des ensembles. Alors un cône pointé de $R$ et $E$ est un élément de la fonction qui envoie $R$ et $E$ à l'ensemble de sous-modules de $\mathrm{Nonneg}$ appliqué à $R$ et $E$.
PointedCone.comapdefMathlib.Geometry.Convex.Cone.Pointed, function or constant

PointedCone.ofConeCombdefMathlib.Geometry.Convex.Cone.Pointed, function or constant
Wording
the pointed cone of C (from the name)
English
Let $C$ be an element of $\wp E$ such that $C$ is nonempty and for all elements $x_{4}$ of $E$, if $x_{4}$ is an element of $C$, then for all elements $y$ of $E$, if $y$ is an element of $C$, then for all elements $a$ of $R$, if $0 \leq a$, then for all elements $b$ of $R$, if $0 \leq b$, then $\HSMulhSMulMACRoAL {a}{x_{4}}+ \HSMulhSMulMACRoAL {b}{y}$ is an element of $C$. Then the pointed cone of $C$ is a pointed cone of $R$ and $E$.
Formula
$C \in \wp E , \operatorname{Nonempty} (C), \forall x_{4} \in E . \MembershipmemMACRoAA {C}{x_{4}}\rightarrow (\forall y \in E . \MembershipmemMACRoAA {C}{y}\rightarrow (\forall a \in R . 0 \leq a \rightarrow (\forall b \in R . 0 \leq b \rightarrow \MembershipmemMACRoAA {C}{\HSMulhSMulMACRoAT {a}{x_{4}}+ \HSMulhSMulMACRoAT {b}{y}})))\vdash \operatorname{ofConeComb} (C , \mathrm{nonempty} , \mathrm{coneComb})\in \mathrm{PointedCone} (R , E)$
French
Soit $C$ un élément de $\wp E$ tel que $C$ soit nonempty et pour tous des éléments $x_{4}$ de $E$, si $x_{4}$ est un élément de $C$, alors pour tous des éléments $y$ de $E$, si $y$ est un élément de $C$, alors pour tous des éléments $a$ de $R$, si $0 \leq a$, alors pour tous des éléments $b$ de $R$, si $0 \leq b$, alors $\HSMulhSMulMACRoAL {a}{x_{4}}+ \HSMulhSMulMACRoAL {b}{y}$ soit un élément de $C$. Mathlib.Geometry.Convex.Cone.Simplicial

PointedCone.IsSimplicialdefMathlib.Geometry.Convex.Cone.Simplicial, predicate
Wording
C is simplicial (from the docstring)
English
Let $C$ be a pointed cone of $R$ and $M$. Then $C$ is simplicial, if there exists an element $s$ of $\wp M$, such that $s$ is finite, $\mathrm{LinearIndepOn}$ holds for $R$, $\mathrm{id}$ and $s$ and the cone hull of $R$ and $s$ is equal to $C$.
Formula
$C \in \mathrm{PointedCone} (R , M)\vdash \operatorname{IsSimplicial} (C):\Leftrightarrow \exists s \in \wp M . \operatorname{Finite} (s)\wedge (\mathrm{LinearIndepOn} (R , \mathrm{id} , s)\wedge (\EqtMACRoJ {\operatorname{hull} (R , s)}{C}))$
French
Soit $C$ un cône pointé de $R$ et $M$. Alors $C$ est simplicial s'il existe un élément $s$ de $\wp M$ tel que $s$ soit fini, $\mathrm{LinearIndepOn}$ tienne pour $R$, $\mathrm{id}$ et $s$ et l'enveloppe de cônes de $R$ et $s$ soit égale à $C$.

Then $s$ is an orthocentric system, if there exists a triangle $t$ of $\Real$ and $P$, such that the orthocenter of $t$ is not an element of the range of $\operatorname{points}$ applied to $t$ and $s$ is equal to $\operatorname{insert}$ applied to the orthocenter of $t$ and the range of $\operatorname{points}$ applied to $t$.

French

Let $s$ be a simplex of $P$ and $n$. Wording

Mathlib.Geometry.Manifold.ContMDiff.Defs

ContDiffWithinAtPropdefMathlib.Geometry.Manifold.ContMDiff.Defs, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $I$ be a model with corner of $E$ and $H$. Let $\mathrm{I'}$ be a model with corner of $\mathrm{E'}$ and $\mathrm{H'}$. Let $n$ be an element of $\mathrm{WithTop}$ applied to the set of natural numbers. Let $f$ be a function from elements of $H$ to elements of $\mathrm{H'}$. Let $s$ be an element of $\wp H$. Let $x_{8}$ be an element of $H$. Then $\mathrm{ContDiffWithinAtProp}$ holds for $I$, $\mathrm{I'}$, $n$, $f$, $s$ and $x_{8}$, if $\mathrm{ContDiffWithinAt}$ holds for $x$, $n$, $\operatorname{comp}$ applied to $\operatorname{toFun'}$ applied to $\mathrm{I'}$ and $\operatorname{comp}$ applied to $f$ and $\operatorname{toFun}$ applied to the inverse of $I$, $\operatorname{inter}$ applied to the preimage of $\operatorname{toFun}$ applied to the inverse of $I$ and $s$ and the range of $\operatorname{toFun'}$ applied to $I$ and the function of $I$ and $x_{8}$.
Formula
$I \in \mathrm{ModelWithCorners} (x , E , H), \mathrm{I'} \in \mathrm{ModelWithCorners} (x , \mathrm{E'} , \mathrm{H'}), n \in \mathrm{WithTop} (\mathrm{ENat}), f \in H \rightarrow \mathrm{H'} , s \in \wp H , x_{8} \in H \vdash \mathrm{ContDiffWithinAtProp} (I , \mathrm{I'} , n , f , s , x_{8}):\Leftrightarrow \mathrm{ContDiffWithinAt} (x , n , \operatorname{comp} (\operatorname{toFun'} (\mathrm{I'}), \operatorname{comp} (f , \operatorname{toFun} (\operatorname{symm} (I)))), \operatorname{inter} (\operatorname{preimage} (\operatorname{toFun} (\operatorname{symm} (I)), s), \operatorname{range} (\operatorname{toFun'} (I))), \operatorname{toFun'} (I , x_{8}))$
French
Soit $I$ un modèle avec du coin de $E$ et $H$. Soit $\mathrm{I'}$ un modèle avec du coin de $\mathrm{E'}$ et $\mathrm{H'}$. Soit $n$ un élément de $\mathrm{WithTop}$ appliqué à l'ensemble de nombres naturels. Soit $f$ une fonction des éléments de $H$ à des éléments de $\mathrm{H'}$. Soit $s$ un élément de $\wp H$. Soit $x_{8}$ un élément de $H$. Alors $\mathrm{ContDiffWithinAtProp}$ tient pour $I$, $\mathrm{I'}$, $n$, $f$, $s$ et $x_{8}$ si $\mathrm{ContDiffWithinAt}$ tient pour $x$, $n$, $\operatorname{comp}$ appliqué à $\operatorname{toFun'}$ appliqué à $\mathrm{I'}$ et $\operatorname{comp}$ appliqué à $f$ et $\operatorname{toFun}$ appliqué à l'inverse de $I$, $\operatorname{inter}$ appliqué à la préimage de $\operatorname{toFun}$ appliqué à l'inverse de $I$ et $s$ et l'image de $\operatorname{toFun'}$ appliqué à $I$ et la fonction de $I$ et $x_{8}$.
ContMDiffdefMathlib.Geometry.Manifold.ContMDiff.Defs, predicate
Wording

ContMDiffAtdefMathlib.Geometry.Manifold.ContMDiff.Defs, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $I$ be a model with corner of $E$ and $H$. Let $\mathrm{I'}$ be a model with corner of $\mathrm{E'}$ and $\mathrm{H'}$. Let $n$ be an element of $\mathrm{WithTop}$ applied to the set of natural numbers. Let $f$ be a function from elements of $M$ to elements of $\mathrm{M'}$. Let $x_{12}$ be an element of $M$. Then $\mathrm{ContMDiffAt}$ holds for $I$, $\mathrm{I'}$, $n$, $f$ and $x_{12}$, if $\mathrm{ContMDiffWithinAt}$ holds for $I$, $\mathrm{I'}$, $n$, $f$, the universal set and $x_{12}$.

$I \in \mathrm{ModelWithCorners} (x , E , H), \mathrm{I'} \in \mathrm{ModelWithCorners} (x , \mathrm{E'} , \mathrm{H'}), n \in \mathrm{WithTop} (\mathrm{ENat}), f \in M \rightarrow \mathrm{M'} , x_{12} \in M \vdash \mathrm{ContMDiffAt} (I , \mathrm{I'} , n , f , x_{12}):\Leftrightarrow \mathrm{ContMDiffWithinAt} (I , \mathrm{I'} , n , f , \operatorname{univ} , x_{12})$

the composition continuous linear equivalence of I and e (from the name)
English
Let $I$ be a model with corner of $E$ and $H$. Let $M$ be a set. Let $e$ be a continuous linear equivalence of $E$ and $F$. Then the composition continuous linear equivalence of $I$ and $e$ is a diffeomorphism of $M$ and $n$.
Formula
$I \in \mathrm{ModelWithCorners} (x , E , H), M \in \mathrm{Set} , e \in \mathrm{ContinuousLinearEquiv} (\operatorname{id} (x), E , F)\vdash \operatorname{toTransContinuousLinearEquiv} (I , M , e)\in \mathrm{Diffeomorph} (I , \operatorname{transContinuousLinearEquiv} (I , e), M , M , n)$
French
Soit $I$ un modèle avec du coin de $E$ et $H$. Soit $M$ un ensemble. Soit $e$ une équivalence linéaire continue de $E$ et $F$. Alors l'équivalence linéaire continue de compositions de $I$ et $e$ est un difféomorphisme de $M$ et $n$.

English

IsDiffImmersionAtdefMathlib.Geometry.Manifold.ImmersionDiff, predicate
Wording
no wording
English
Let $I$ be a model with corner of $E$ and $H$. Let $\mathrm{I'}$ be a model with corner of $\mathrm{E'}$ and $\mathrm{H'}$. Let $f$ be a function from elements of $M$ to elements of $\mathrm{M'}$. Then $\mathrm{IsDiffImmersionAt}$ holds for $I$, $\mathrm{I'}$, $f$ and $x_{12}$, if $\mathrm{mfderiv}$ applied to $I$, $\mathrm{I'}$, $f$ and $x_{12}$ has left inverses.
Formula

tangentBundleModelSpaceDiffeomorphdefMathlib.Geometry.Manifold.MFDeriv.Tangent, function or constant
Wording
the tangent bundle model space diffeomorphism of I and n (from the name)
English
Let $I$ be a model with corner of $E$ and $H$. Let $n$ be a natural number. Then the tangent bundle model space diffeomorphism of $I$ and $n$ is a diffeomorphism of the set of model products of $H$ and $E$ and $\operatorname{some}$ applied to $n$.
Formula
$I \in \mathrm{ModelWithCorners} (x , E , H), n \in \mathrm{ENat} \vdash \mathrm{tangentBundleModelSpaceDiffeomorph} (I , n)\in \mathrm{Diffeomorph} (\operatorname{tangent} (I), \operatorname{prod} (I , \mathrm{modelWithCornersSelf} (x , E)), \mathrm{TangentBundle} (I , H), \mathrm{ModelProd} (H , E), \operatorname{some} (n))$
French
Soit $I$ un modèle avec du coin de $E$ et $H$. Soit $n$ un nombre naturel. Alors le difféomorphisme d'espaces de modèles de fibrés tangent de $I$ et $n$ est un difféomorphisme de l'ensemble de produits de modèles de $H$ et $E$ et $\operatorname{some}$ appliqué à $n$.
